I don't know, I just end up second-guessing routine quality calls that I used to make without any trouble.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: I don't know if I'd use that word. I'm just... stressed, I guess. Like I said, it's just a lot of pressure at the plant right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: I guess... maybe. I've sort of... pulled back from some of the meetings I'm expected to attend. Just trying to avoid the back-and-forth, I suppose. But... well, I've missed some internal deadlines lately because of it. My work record has taken a hit. \n\nAnd... I don't know, it's just spilling over. Like I said, my husband and I are arguing more, and I'm not really keeping up with our usual evening routines at home either. It's just... a lot right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't say sad, exactly. Just... tired, mostly. Tired of dealing with all this.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding the new operations manager himself, or is it just the situations or tasks that he is involved in?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I haven't thought about it like that. I guess I just try to keep my head down and stay out of the way as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts or memories about work that keep popping into your head when you're not trying to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose. I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. \n\nI come home all wound up, and... I'll just lie awake at night, replaying those meetings in my head. Just hearing him pick apart the inspection procedures I've used for years. It just stays with me. And then the next day I'm... second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings when you're at work or thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Detached? No... I don't think so. I know exactly what's going on around me. I just... I don't really feel like that, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of intense fear or discomfort at work or when thinking about work?\n\nPatient: No. I wouldn't call it intense fear. Just... stressed, I guess. But no panic or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work?\n\nPatient: About two months, I suppose. Right around the time the new operations manager came in and started... changing how I do things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members outside of your husband since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: Not really with other family or friends, no. Mostly just... with my husband. We've been arguing more lately. I just haven't been keeping up with our usual evening routines, so... yeah. \n\nAnd I guess... well, my work record has taken a hit, too. I've missed a few internal deadlines. I just kind of... pull back from the meetings I'm expected to attend. But as for other people... no, I haven't noticed anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding meetings or situations at work because you fear something might happen?\n\nPatient: No, it's not... fear. I just... I don't want to deal with it. So I don't go.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble enjoying activities or hobbies that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I haven't really... felt like doing much of anything lately. Just... watching TV, maybe. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific work-related tasks or situations that you're avoiding because they remind you of the conflicts with the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's not about being reminded of anything. I just... keep to myself. I do my inspections and just... avoid the meetings, like I said. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are there things related to work, such as people, places, or objects, that you try to avoid because they remind you of the conflicts with the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: I... no. Like I just said, it's not about being reminded of anything. I don't avoid places or objects or whatever. I just go in, do my shift, and go home. That's it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I'm just... stressed, I guess. Like I said, it's just a lot of pressure at the plant right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding certain tasks or situations at work since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: I guess... maybe. I've sort of... pulled back from some of the meetings I'm expected to attend. Just trying to avoid the back-and-forth, I suppose. But... well, I've missed some internal deadlines lately because of it. My work record has taken a hit. \n\nAnd... I don't know, it's just spilling over. Like I said, my husband and I are arguing more, and I'm not really keeping up with our usual evening routines at home either. It's just... a lot right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don't... I wouldn't say sad, exactly. Just... tired, mostly. Tired of dealing with all this.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding the new operations manager himself, or is it just the situations or tasks that he is involved in?\n\nPatient: I don't really know. I haven't... I haven't thought about it like that. I guess I just try to keep my head down and stay out of the way as much as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you have thoughts or memories about work that keep popping into your head when you're not trying to think about them?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I suppose. I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. \n\nI come home all wound up, and... I'll just lie awake at night, replaying those meetings in my head. Just hearing him pick apart the inspection procedures I've used for years. It just stays with me. And then the next day I'm... second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you're detached from yourself or your surroundings when you're at work or thinking about work?\n\nPatient: Detached? No... I don't think so. I know exactly what's going on around me. I just... I don't really feel like that, no.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of intense fear or discomfort at work or when thinking about work?\n\nPatient: No. I wouldn't call it intense fear. Just... stressed, I guess. But no panic or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work?\n\nPatient: About two months, I suppose. Right around the time the new operations manager came in and started... changing how I do things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your relationships with friends or family members outside of your husband since the new operations manager started?\n\nPatient: Not really with other family or friends, no. Mostly just... with my husband. We've been arguing more lately. I just haven't been keeping up with our usual evening routines, so... yeah. \n\nAnd I guess... well, my work record has taken a hit, too. I've missed a few internal deadlines. I just kind of... pull back from the meetings I'm expected to attend. But as for other people... no, I haven't noticed anything.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding meetings or situations at work because you fear something might happen?\n\nPatient: No, it's not... fear. I just... I don't want to deal with it. So I don't go.\n\nDoctor: Are you having trouble enjoying activities or hobbies that you normally find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I guess I haven't really... felt like doing much of anything lately. Just... watching TV, maybe. I haven't really thought about it.\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific work-related tasks or situations that you're avoiding because they remind you of the conflicts with the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's not about being reminded of anything. I just... keep to myself. I do my inspections and just... avoid the meetings, like I said. That's all.\n\nDoctor: Are there things related to work, such as people, places, or objects, that you try to avoid because they remind you of the conflicts with the new operations manager?\n\nPatient: I... no. Like I just said, it's not about being reminded of anything. I don't avoid places or objects or whatever. I just go in, do my shift, and go home. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions with coworkers outside of the meetings you've been pulling back from?\n\nPatient: No. I mean... I never really socialized much with them anyway. So... nothing's really changed there. I just do my job.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work because of how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't taken days off. But... I guess my work has been affected a bit. I've missed a couple of internal deadlines. And, well, like I mentioned, I've been pulling back from those meetings I'm supposed to be at. It's just... it's bleeding into things at home, too. My husband and I are arguing more lately. I just haven't had the energy for our usual evening stuff.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant problems with your ability to perform your job or maintain relationships outside of work due to your current stress and anxiety?\n\nPatient: I... I thought I just said that. Um... yeah, my work has taken a hit. Like I said, the deadlines, the meetings... I'm not doing what I'm supposed to. And... at home, it's just... the arguments with my husband. And I'm just not doing the things we usually do in the evenings.
